## UX 2050 (Jakob Nielsen keynote)

- Channel: NNgroup
- Published: 2021-04-23
- Length: 36 min
- URL: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y4J6JR9jEUk
- Theme: Iconic studios and designers: interviews and talks (Awwwards jury, Locomotive, Ueno, Pentagram, Collins, Nielsen)

[0:00] - Jakob Nielsen recently shared his prediction for what UX looks like in 2050. Here's his keynote speech from our virtual conference in 2020. - Well, thank you very much, Sarah. And thank you everybody for coming, joining us in particular, of course, if it's in the middle of the night where you are. Since this is a February conference, I thought I would start by just briefly talk about Numa Pompilius. And he lived 2,700 years ago. And Numa Pompilius is responsible for the months of January and February because before him the year only had 10 months in the calendar. And that's actually why the months of September, October, November and December are named after number seven, eight, nine, and 10 in Latin because those were the numbers of those months at the time. And then Numa Pompilius introduced two more months and not at the end, put them at the beginning. So the numbering scheme was messed up and also said that it was February which I think is a usability abomination of a month.

[1:01] So that has caused so much trouble for all the little children for 2,700 years in learning the calendar so we can blame him for that. I just mentioned this story because it shows you how design decisions can sometimes last for a very long time though I don't think most of the designs we work on will still be here in 2,700 years. But even so that's why it makes sense to look ahead and talk about sort of a little bit of a longer term picture. And they're really two different ways we can consider problems. And so by way of example, I'm showing you two planets. We can look at planets, study them bottom up like earth was studied. Like first you get hold of like one rock and you can kind of how's this rock. And you then you maybe look at one volcano, one Island to build up to a bigger, bigger understanding of that planet until you get to things like continental drift and what have you. On the other hand, Mars was studied a top-down. And so in the beginning, people just knew it was a red planet and we still actually don't have rocks from Mars like under the microscope in the laboratories here, there are some missions

First, I always like to, when try to look ahead, try this mirror to flip it and look back the same amount of years to get us an idea of what can happen at this timeframe we're looking at. [40:46] test three users obviously you don't have that much recruiting to do which is great and you will find generally speaking all the problems that you need to find you know as many problems as you can fix or more problems that you can fix by testing three users if you test more users you will find more problems but the fact is you don't need to find more problems than that you'll find you'll find very serious problems with testing just a few users so keep it as simple as possible you don't need a lab you don't need two-way two-way glass two-way mirror whatever it's properly called uh screen sharing Works incredibly well now so you just set up screen sharing in another room with a monitor so people can observe you can record it with something like Camtasia screen recorder um you don't need entrance and exit questions because you're only testing three people so there's no there's no validity to anything statistical you could you could gather so there's no point in doing it you don't need to create a big honken report historically what would happen with usability testing is you'd bring in an outside consultant who would run the usability test they would then do all the observations

[42:50] book One start earlier than you think makes sense people always want to wait to start doing usability testing and one of the reasons there are whole bunch of reasons for this but one of them is that you always have a better version in your head than you have available to show you always know things that you're going to fix you always know things that you're going to improve you just haven't had time to do them yet so people say well why should we show people this version that in a week is going to be much better why don't we wait until it's better and then show it to them because if we don't we're going to waste our time and their time having them point out problems to us that we already know exist the problem is that's not what you're looking for they're going to point out to you the problems that you don't know exist because they're going to look at it differently than you are um and the other thing is that the earlier you start testing the the sooner you discover what these problems are so that you don't build them into your product you know the the longer you wait to discover these problems the more expensive it is in time and money for you to go back and fix them okay and
